## Pitfalls

### Collapsing valid, licit and fruitful

- **"Invalid" and "illicit" as two words for "wrong".** Invalid: nothing happened; illicit: something happened unlawfully. A suspended priest's Mass, a rewritten preface with the consecratory words intact, a 1962 Mass without the reserved permission: valid and illicit. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-matter-form-minister-intention.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-what-the-liturgy-is.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-twentieth-century-reform-and-the-disputes-over-it.md))*
- **"Valid but unfruitful means nothing happened."** Christ is received in a communion in mortal sin, sacramentally only; baptism, confirmation and order still imprint the character, so there is something to revive. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-ex-opere-operato-character-and-fruitfulness.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-communion-reservation-adoration.md))*
- **"*Ex opere operato* means it works no matter what."** It works whatever the minister's holiness, but only with matter, form and intention, and bears fruit in an adult only without an obstacle. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-ex-opere-operato-character-and-fruitfulness.md))*
- **A breach of the fast, or a baptism without founded hope of upbringing, read as invalid.** Each touches liceity only. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-necessity-infants-and-baptism-of-desire-and-blood.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-communion-reservation-adoration.md))*
- **A "grave matter" in *Redemptionis Sacramentum* read as an invalid Mass.** RS's categories rank gravity; validity stays with matter, form, minister and intention. *([7.2](lessons/07-02-liturgical-language-and-ars-celebrandi.md))*
- **General absolution outside can. 961 read as invalid.** The canon has no invalidating clause; validity turns on the penitent's disposition and intention to confess (can. 962 §1). *([4.1](lessons/04-01-penance-the-sacrament-and-its-history.md))*
- **Forgetting that ecclesiastical law can invalidate:** a faculty decides valid absolution and Latin presbyteral confirmation; form and delegation decide a marriage's validity; the ordained invalidly attempt marriage (can. 1087). *([2.3](lessons/02-03-confirmation.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-penance-the-sacrament-and-its-history.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-holy-orders-the-three-degrees.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md))*
- **"Only a bishop confirms validly" or "any priest can confirm".** Trent defines the bishop the *ordinary* minister; a Latin presbyter without the faculty confirms invalidly, unless the Church supplies it (can. 144 §2). *([2.3](lessons/02-03-confirmation.md))*

### Giving a school's explanation the level of the definition

- **Instrumental causality, accidents without a subject, the Thomist character, reviviscence, or one account of how the one sacrifice is present, presented as dogma.** Trent defined *that* (rung 1); the *how* is theology, mostly rung 5 (reviviscence of baptism rung 4). *([1.1](lessons/01-01-what-a-sacrament-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-ex-opere-operato-character-and-fruitfulness.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-real-presence-and-transubstantiation.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md))*
- **"Trent defined accidents without a subject, as Aristotle teaches"; "a Catholic must be a Thomist".** Trent says *species*; *Mysterium Fidei* 24 ties the formulas to no school. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-real-presence-and-transubstantiation.md))*
- **"*Mediator Dei* 115 or the Catechism's 're-presents' made one account of the sacrifice Church teaching."** All accounts are freely disputed. *([3.4](lessons/03-04-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md))*
- **"The words of institution alone consecrate, at the instant completed" stated as defined.** Common Latin teaching, rung 4; Florence's form with Trent XIII ch. 3 is at least rung 3. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-the-roman-rite-and-the-eastern-rites.md))*
- **The soldier-and-maturity account of confirmation as defined.** LG 11's "special strength" is rung 3; the combat imagery reached the schoolmen through a forgery. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-confirmation.md))*
- **The spouses as ministers stated as dogma.** Rung 4, Latin common teaching, reported beside the Eastern view. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md))*

### Treating a defined point as opinion

- **Causing grace a "Thomist opinion"; the character a "scholastic theory"; transubstantiation "one theory among several"; the sacrifice "opinion" because its *how* is open.** Each *that* is rung 1. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-what-a-sacrament-is.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-ex-opere-operato-character-and-fruitfulness.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-real-presence-and-transubstantiation.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md))*
- **"A twelfth-century count makes the seven a medieval opinion."** Rung 1 since Trent, taught by the ordinary magisterium before it. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-why-seven.md))*
- **"Instituted by Christ" opened for any one sacrament.** Denying Christ's authorship of any of the seven contradicts rung 1; only the *manner* is rung 5. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-why-seven.md))*
- **"Nothing here is defined, so it is all exegesis."** XXII can. 2 binds the sense of "Do this" at rung 1. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-the-institution-and-the-earliest-witness.md))*
- **"Nothing necessary is lost under one kind" as pastoral reassurance; "Trent merely tolerates attrition"; water in baptism "a changeable discipline"; concupiscence "original sin left over".** Each contradicts a rung-1 canon. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-baptism-the-rite-and-its-effects.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-communion-reservation-adoration.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-penance-the-sacrament-and-its-history.md))*
- **"Indulgences an optional opinion."** The power to grant them is rung 1; only seeking one is free. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-indulgences-as-a-practice.md))*

### Overstating a level

- **Florence's Decree for the Armenians as a definition of matter and form.** A practical instruction, changeable, as 1947 showed; its doctrinal statements at least rung 3. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-why-seven.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-baptism-the-rite-and-its-effects.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-holy-orders-the-three-degrees.md))*
- **"Trent defined baptism of desire."** It presupposes it in a chapter and drafts a canon around it: at least rung 3, not rung 1. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-necessity-infants-and-baptism-of-desire-and-blood.md))*
- **"Limbo was dogma."** Never defined: rung 5. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-necessity-infants-and-baptism-of-desire-and-blood.md))*
- **"Lateran IV defined transubstantiation"; "Constance declared remanence heretical"; "the church has defined that John 6 is about the Eucharist".** A profession using the participle; a global censure; Trent left John 6 open. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-the-institution-and-the-earliest-witness.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-controversies-that-forced-precision.md))*
- ***Ordinatio Sacerdotalis* "a solemn definition"; the 1995 response "settled" it.** At least rung 2, rung 1 against rung 2 disputed, the response's claim disputed. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-the-reservation-of-priestly-ordination.md))*
- **"Canon 7 solemnly defined indissolubility."** Rung 1 as worded; what it defined is disputed; absolute indissolubility is at least rung 2. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md))*
- ***Amoris Laetitia*, *Summorum Pontificum*, *Traditionis Custodes* or *Fiducia Supplicans* read as defining something.** AL and the Buenos Aires texts rung 3; SP and TC law; FS's account of blessings at most rung 3, its permission practical. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-nullity-dissolution-and-the-pastoral-debates.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-twentieth-century-reform-and-the-disputes-over-it.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-sacramentals-and-popular-devotion.md))*
- **SC's norms as irreformable; *Quo Primum* as making the 1570 Missal irreformable; the 2001 judgment as making the narrative optional; can. 6 making the Roman Canon the only lawful prayer.** Law, law, a single-case doctrinal judgment, and "free from error" only. *([6.1](lessons/06-01-what-the-liturgy-is.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-the-roman-rite-and-the-eastern-rites.md))*
- **The seal as a defined dogma; celibacy as dogma; the Directory's criteria as dogma; the 2025 letter as closing the diaconate.** Law and at least rung 3; law; changeable direction; a commission is not magisterium. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-penance-the-sacrament-and-its-history.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-holy-orders-the-three-degrees.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-reservation-of-priestly-ordination.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-sacramentals-and-popular-devotion.md))*
- **A Catechism summary or a historical aside treated as adding weight** (CCC 1170 on Nicaea; CCC 1471 "living or dead"). *([4.2](lessons/04-02-indulgences-as-a-practice.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-liturgical-year-and-the-liturgy-of-the-hours.md))*

### Understating a level

- **"Matter and form is a school's vocabulary, so the essentials are negotiable."** Intention and true water are rung 1; what the supreme authority fixes for validity binds (can. 841). *([1.2](lessons/01-02-matter-form-minister-intention.md))*
- **"Priests only" for anointing as law the Church could relax.** The proper minister rung 1; priests-only validity at least rung 2. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-anointing-of-the-sick.md))*
- **OS "a discipline the next pope could lift"; the female diaconate "settled" either way.** At least rung 2; and open, rung 5. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-the-reservation-of-priestly-ordination.md))*
- **"SC is only pastoral, so optional"; "the Sunday obligation is only a custom now"; *Sacramentum Ordinis* "a rubric"; the 2001 judgment "a pastoral tolerance"; AL "only advice".** At least rung 3; law with its gravity taught (rung 3); binding for validity; a rung-3 doctrinal judgment; rung 3. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-holy-orders-the-three-degrees.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-nullity-dissolution-and-the-pastoral-debates.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-what-the-liturgy-is.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-liturgical-year-and-the-liturgy-of-the-hours.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-the-roman-rite-and-the-eastern-rites.md))*
- **"Its indirect wording left remarriage after adultery open."** XXIV can. 7 is rung 1 as worded. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md))*
- **Relic veneration "merely tolerated".** Trent teaches it. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-sacramentals-and-popular-devotion.md))*

### Doctrine and discipline

- **The age of confirmation, the order of initiation, the eucharistic hour, the Easter dates, the age of first communion, the cup's distribution, celibacy, the date of Easter, the calendar, the Office's shape and obligation, indulgence norms** are law or discipline: binding, changeable, no rung. The canon that defines a precept (XIII can. 9) defines that it binds, not its terms. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-confirmation.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-communion-reservation-adoration.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-indulgences-as-a-practice.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-holy-orders-the-three-degrees.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-the-liturgical-year-and-the-liturgy-of-the-hours.md))*
- **"The Lateran precept makes confession obligatory."** Confessing grave sin is of divine right (XIV can. 6); Lateran fixed the yearly minimum. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-penance-the-sacrament-and-its-history.md))*
- **"Canonical form is divine law."** Ecclesiastical law, universal only from 1908, dispensable. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md))*
- **"Nicaea fixed the full-moon rule."** One day for all, no computus. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-the-liturgical-year-and-the-liturgy-of-the-hours.md))*
- **"Moving the Ascension to Sunday changed the faith."** Which day keeps a feast is liturgical law. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-the-liturgical-year-and-the-liturgy-of-the-hours.md))*

### Dispensation and substance

- **The Church's power over the sacraments read as unlimited, or as nil.** She may ordain or change what concerns their dispensation, "their substance being untouched" (Trent XXI ch. 2, at least rung 3): the cup, the porrection, the 1971 and 1972 rites, the Missal of 1969 are dispensation; who can receive priestly ordination is placed in the substance ("no authority whatsoever"). *([1.2](lessons/01-02-matter-form-minister-intention.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-why-seven.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-communion-reservation-adoration.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-reservation-of-priestly-ordination.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-what-the-liturgy-is.md))*
- **A priest's authority over the rite confused with the Church's.** No one, "not even a priest", may add, remove or change anything (SC 22 §3); the Church may; no one may alter what Christ fixed. *([6.1](lessons/06-01-what-the-liturgy-is.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-twentieth-century-reform-and-the-disputes-over-it.md))*
- **"Trent VII can. 13 forbade the reform."** It bars change by "any pastor whatever"; the same council left the missal to the pope. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-twentieth-century-reform-and-the-disputes-over-it.md))*
- **"*Sacramentum Ordinis* condemned the medieval rite."** It decided "at least for the future", without retroactive force. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-holy-orders-the-three-degrees.md))*

### Sacrament and sacramental

- **A sacramental as a small sacrament working *ex opere operato*.** An exegetical error, graded strictly: it works *ex opere operantis Ecclesiae*. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-ex-opere-operato-character-and-fruitfulness.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-sacramentals-and-popular-devotion.md))*
- **"Not *ex opere operato*" read as "only as good as my feelings".** The prayer is the Church's, its power from the paschal mystery. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-sacramentals-and-popular-devotion.md))*
- **A blessed oil used outside the sacrament treated as the anointing, or lay anointing as a permitted sacramental.** At most a sacramental, and *Ecclesiae de mysterio* 9 forbids the non-ordained any anointing of the sick with any oil. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-anointing-of-the-sick.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-sacramentals-and-popular-devotion.md))*
- **Monastic profession or holy water counted among the sacraments; "like a sacrament" (LG 1) as an eighth.** *([1.1](lessons/01-01-what-a-sacrament-is.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-why-seven.md))*
- **An indulgence treated as a sacrament,** with matter, form and the three verdicts: it is a grant, gained in full, in part or not at all. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-indulgences-as-a-practice.md))*
- **Superstition:** expecting the result from the procedure rather than from God through the Church's prayer and one's own. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-sacramentals-and-popular-devotion.md))*

### Nullity, dissolution and divorce

- **"Annulment is a Catholic divorce"; "a declaration says the marriage failed".** Nullity says no bond ever came to be; something was missing on the day. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-nullity-dissolution-and-the-pastoral-debates.md))*
- **"The Pauline privilege annuls", or "baptism dissolves the marriage".** It ends a real natural bond when the baptized party remarries (can. 1143 §1). *([5.4](lessons/05-04-nullity-dissolution-and-the-pastoral-debates.md))*
- **"The pope can dissolve any marriage."** Not a marriage both ratified and consummated (can. 1141; at least rung 2). *([5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-nullity-dissolution-and-the-pastoral-debates.md))*
- **"*Mitis Iudex* added grounds."** It changed who judges and how fast. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-nullity-dissolution-and-the-pastoral-debates.md))*
- **Henry VIII sought a divorce.** He sought a nullity, and Clement VII ruled the marriage valid. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-nullity-dissolution-and-the-pastoral-debates.md))*
- **"The priest marries the couple" (Latin), or forgetting the Eastern priest's blessing is required for validity.** *([5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md))*

### Priesthood and diaconate

- **"A deacon is a junior priest."** Ordained "not unto priesthood but unto ministry"; he cannot consecrate, absolve or anoint; yet a degree of the sacrament, with a character. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-holy-orders-the-three-degrees.md))*
- **"Deacons act *in persona Christi capitis*."** The pre-2009 can. 1008 said so; can. 1009 §3 now gives that capacity to bishops and presbyters and the diakonia to deacons, in two positive clauses. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-holy-orders-the-three-degrees.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-reservation-of-priestly-ordination.md))*
- **Running the two questions together:** OS answers priestly ordination; no act answers the diaconate; the level disputes about OS do not reach it. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-the-reservation-of-priestly-ordination.md))*
- **"Refuting the nuptial symbol refutes the teaching."** The symbol is fittingness; the teaching rests on Christ's act. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-the-reservation-of-priestly-ordination.md))*

### The reformed Missal: validity and prudence

- **Running three questions together:** could the Church reform (settled, at least rung 3), is the reformed rite valid and lawful (settled), was it done well (freely disputed). The older books' freedom is a fourth, and is law. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-twentieth-century-reform-and-the-disputes-over-it.md))*
- **"Criticizing the reform is disloyal"** shrinks what is open; **"the reformed Missal's validity is debatable"** opens what is settled. TC art. 3 §1 asks not to deny validity and legitimacy, not to think every change prudent. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-twentieth-century-reform-and-the-disputes-over-it.md))*
- **"The council ordered Mass facing the people."** SC is silent on it. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-twentieth-century-reform-and-the-disputes-over-it.md))*
- **"Trent condemned the vernacular."** It judged it not expedient everywhere and anathematized only "vernacular only". *([7.2](lessons/07-02-liturgical-language-and-ars-celebrandi.md))*
- **"*Liturgiam Authenticam* still governs every word" or "*Magnum Principium* abolished it".** *([7.2](lessons/07-02-liturgical-language-and-ars-celebrandi.md))*

### Volunteering a rebuttal

- **Stating another tradition and then answering it.** Lutheran, Reformed, Anglican, Baptist and Orthodox positions are stated as their own confessions and theologians state them, with authors and documents, and left standing; the argument is [`apologetics-catholic-claims`](../apologetics-catholic-claims/syllabus.md)'s. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-ex-opere-operato-character-and-fruitfulness.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-why-seven.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-necessity-infants-and-baptism-of-desire-and-blood.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-controversies-that-forced-precision.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-anointing-of-the-sick.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-reservation-of-priestly-ordination.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-nullity-dissolution-and-the-pastoral-debates.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-the-roman-rite-and-the-eastern-rites.md))*
- **Stating it below its strength:** "the Reformers made the sacraments bare symbols" (Luther's Small Catechism: baptism works forgiveness); "Zwingli denied any presence" (his 1531 *Exposition*); "the Orthodox reject the seven" (their confessions teach seven); the infant-baptism objection as "the Reformation's" (it is Anabaptist and Baptist); "Ratramnus was a Zwinglian". *([1.3](lessons/01-03-ex-opere-operato-character-and-fruitfulness.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-why-seven.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-necessity-infants-and-baptism-of-desire-and-blood.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-controversies-that-forced-precision.md))*
- **Sorting positions by the wrong question** (figure or truth) so that real differences vanish. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-controversies-that-forced-precision.md))*

### Words that mislead

- **"Source and summit" as SC 10's phrase about the liturgy** (it is CCC 1324's rendering of LG 11, on the Eucharistic sacrifice); **"active participation" as activity or jobs**; **"commemoration" as mental recollection**; **"represented" or "unbloody" as only symbolic**; **"propitiatory" as winning over a reluctant God**; **"real" as making other presences unreal**; **"not as in a place" as "only spiritually"**. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-the-institution-and-the-earliest-witness.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-real-presence-and-transubstantiation.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-the-sacrifice-of-the-mass.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-what-the-liturgy-is.md))*
- **"Extreme unction" as the last rites for the dying only, or "Vatican II abolished it".** SC 73 dates it from the start of danger and calls the new name more fitting; viaticum is the sacrament of the dying. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-anointing-of-the-sick.md))*
- **"300 days" as 300 days off purgatory**; **"the usual conditions" as a checklist**. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-indulgences-as-a-practice.md))*
- **"This is a great sacrament" (Eph 5:32) as settling matrimony's sacramentality**; ***sacramentum* as only the seven**. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-what-a-sacrament-is.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-matrimony-sacrament-contract-indissolubility.md))*
- **"For many" as teaching that Christ died only for some**; **"dynamic equivalence" as *Comme le prévoit*'s own term**. *([7.2](lessons/07-02-liturgical-language-and-ars-celebrandi.md))*
- **The four-word *lex orandi* tag as the chapter's own wording, or as letting any liturgical text settle doctrine.** *([6.1](lessons/06-01-what-the-liturgy-is.md))*
- **"The Office is the priest's private breviary."** *([6.2](lessons/06-02-the-liturgical-year-and-the-liturgy-of-the-hours.md))*

### Numbering the psalms

- **Looking up a liturgical psalm reference in a Hebrew-numbered Bible.** The GILH, the *Liturgia Horarum* and the Lectionary number by the Greek and Vulgate: "Psalm 94" is Hebrew 95. Convert before you look ([psalm numbering](#psalm-numbering); [`old-testament` 5.1](../old-testament/lessons/05-01-the-psalms.md)). *([6.2](lessons/06-02-the-liturgical-year-and-the-liturgy-of-the-hours.md))*
- **The Douay runs one verse ahead in John 6 from 6:51b** (6:53 = DR 6:54; 6:66 = DR 6:67). *([3.1](lessons/03-01-the-institution-and-the-earliest-witness.md))*

### History read as doctrine, doctrine as history

- **"The Fathers counted seven"; "monastic profession is no sacrament because Dionysius didn't count it".** Counting was not a patristic question; Dionysius's *mysterion* is as broad as Augustine's *sacramentum*. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-why-seven.md))*
- **"Indulgences were sold, and Trent said so before Luther's critics did."** Boniface IX condemned sellers in 1392; Trent's decree (1563) admits the abuses. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-indulgences-as-a-practice.md))*
- **The 1059 oath as dogma, or as a buried scandal.** Its realism was defined, its sensory predicates corrected openly. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-controversies-that-forced-precision.md))*
- **"The pure Mass of the city of Rome."** The Roman Rite absorbed Frankish and other usages and is Roman by reception and authority. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-the-roman-rite-and-the-eastern-rites.md))*
- **A first-century text read as Trent, or Trent's vocabulary read into the earliest witness.** Realist in language, silent about mechanism; weigh each text for what it says, never average the witnesses. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-the-institution-and-the-earliest-witness.md))*

---

## Conventions

- **One card per course**, covering every lesson. The linter checks that every lesson file is cited somewhere on this card.
- **Intuition first**, as in the lessons: a plain-English line before any Latin, Greek or canon.
- **Levels exactly as the lessons mark them.** Where a lesson says "at least rung 3", "rung 1 as worded", "at most rung 3" or "no rung assigned", the card says the same; it never rounds a level up or down. Where the build corrected the syllabus (the seal's penalty now can. 1386 §1; *Apostolicae Curae* an apostolic letter at rung 2; the consecration penalty can. 1387), the card follows the lessons.
- **Defined against explained.** Every doctrine entry separates what an act defines from the school that explains it; the explanation never borrows the definition's level.
- **Other traditions are stated, never answered.** The [Other traditions](#other-traditions-in-their-own-words) entries carry no reply.
- **Headings are anchors.** Renaming a `###` breaks inbound lesson links. Headings are ASCII only (no italics, parentheses, dashes, Greek or diacritics); Latin and Greek terms appear in the body.
- **Boss problems are reserved.** Entries touching boss material stay at reference level: the texts, their levels and the positions are here; numbered-premise reconstructions of ST III q.60 or qq.75-77, which-canon mappings, same-and-different extractions, the Didache-Justin table, the moment-and-agent comparison, rite classifications, the SC-mandate mapping and the essays are not.
- **Modern authors and post-1930 magisterial texts are paraphrased**, cited by work, year and, where verified, section; DH numbers only where the lessons verified them. Trent is quoted in Waterworth's translation, Aquinas in the English Dominican translation, Scripture in the Douay-Rheims (Challoner).
- **No prose dollar signs** (see CLAUDE.md). Length is not capped the way a lesson's is: this is a lookup surface, not a read-through.
